# O1 — Powerlifting then marathon

Complete new proposal, generated by unchanged engine at 560199b. Synthetic; not activated or cleared for training. Exact metadata remains in adjacent JSON.

## Frozen source and assumptions

- Exact event dates and horizon reuse the historical PDF calendar. The original user supplied relative times.
- Training days reuse the historical PDF calendar; all 90-minute weekday caps and 240-minute Sunday caps are new explicit comparison assumptions, not athlete facts.
- Training age is an explicit comparison assumption; first competition is not proof of lifting inexperience.
- Current scalar mileage uses the prior PDF provisional assumption (30 km or midpoint45 km); no six-week history or long-run recovery data is invented.
- The previous marathon is placed on25October solely to make the historical first recovery week executable; exact completion date was not provided.

## Week 1 — Development / Recovery

Priority: Squat. Calendar feasible: true. Review required: true.

- Recent marathon: provisional easy return, with no quality or long-run progression until recovery is confirmed. Rest or shorten if normal easy running is not comfortable.
- Maintain an easy aerobic base for the later marathon while strength has priority. Race-specific running starts after the dated handover and recovery review.
- Confirm recent long-run distance and recovery before extending the endurance-support long run; a past marathon does not establish present long-run readiness.

```json
{
```
```json
  "missedSession": "Skip or move one session only if the next key session and recovery still fit. Do not stack missed quality, heavy work or long runs. After two missed sessions, illness or a week away, review the next block.",
  "liftingEffort": "Warm up to the prescribed effort. RPE governs the suggested kg. If effort overshoots by a point, reduce 2.5–5% and reassess; if still excessive, omit remaining back-offs. Heavy singles are skill practice, not forced maximal attempts.",
  "speedWork": "Accelerate with stable technique. Reduce load or stop when speed or position deteriorates. An easy speed day is not permission to turn it into a heavy day.",
  "athleteJudgement": "Report sustained fatigue, sleep disruption, pain, illness or altered movement. End the provoking work when needed. One difficult session is a reason to adjust that session, not an automatic full deload.",
  "review": "Review completed work every four weeks and after events. Later blocks are conditional on observed recovery and performance; no race result or lifting total is guaranteed."
}
```

```json
}
```

## Week 6 — Specificity / Aerobic foundation

Priority: Squat. Calendar feasible: true. Review required: true.

- Maintain an easy aerobic base for the later marathon while strength has priority. Race-specific running starts after the dated handover and recovery review.
- Confirm recent long-run distance and recovery before extending the endurance-support long run; a past marathon does not establish present long-run readiness.

## Week 8 — Specificity / Aerobic foundation

Priority: Squat. Calendar feasible: true. Review required: true.

- Maintain an easy aerobic base for the later marathon while strength has priority. Race-specific running starts after the dated handover and recovery review.
- Confirm recent long-run distance and recovery before extending the endurance-support long run; a past marathon does not establish present long-run readiness.

```json
  "speedWork": "Accelerate with stable technique. Reduce load or stop when speed or position deteriorates. An easy speed day is not permission to turn it into a heavy day.",

  "review": "Review completed work every four weeks and after events. Later blocks are conditional on observed recovery and performance; no race result or lifting total is guaranteed."
}
```

## Week 11 — Specificity / Aerobic foundation

Priority: Squat. Calendar feasible: true. Review required: true.

- Maintain an easy aerobic base for the later marathon while strength has priority. Race-specific running starts after the dated handover and recovery review.
- Confirm recent long-run distance and recovery before extending the endurance-support long run; a past marathon does not establish present long-run readiness.

```json
}
```

```json
{
  "handoverRecovery": {
    "eventDate": "2027-01-23",
    "sport": "strength",
    "conditional": true,
    "note": "The dated handover is provisional. Confirm actual event recovery and completed training before accepting the next heavy or specific block; elapsed days alone do not clear fatigue."
  },
  "missedSession": "Skip or move one session only if the next key session and recovery still fit. Do not stack missed quality, heavy work or long runs. After two missed sessions, illness or a week away, review the next block.",
  "liftingEffort": "Warm up to the prescribed effort. RPE governs the suggested kg. If effort overshoots by a point, reduce 2.5–5% and reassess; if still excessive, omit remaining back-offs. Heavy singles are skill practice, not forced maximal attempts.",
  "speedWork": "Accelerate with stable technique. Reduce load or stop when speed or position deteriorates. An easy speed day is not permission to turn it into a heavy day.",
  "athleteJudgement": "Report sustained fatigue, sleep disruption, pain, illness or altered movement. End the provoking work when needed. One difficult session is a reason to adjust that session, not an automatic full deload.",
  "review": "Review completed work every four weeks and after events. Later blocks are conditional on observed recovery and performance; no race result or lifting total is guaranteed."
}
```

## Week 17 — Development / Specific preparation

Priority: Running. Calendar feasible: true. Review required: true.

- Post-race / post-event handover review: confirm actual recovery and completed training before accepting the next heavy or specific block. Elapsed recovery days alone do not establish readiness.
- The post-meet marathon build is conditional on restored recovery and completed aerobic support. Confirm actual mileage at the handover before progressing; proposed future kilometres are not evidence that those runs were completed.
- Marathon endurance review: this specific block is not yet supported by substantial weekly mileage and long-run capacity. Review a completion-led run/walk approach, more suitable availability or the race date.
- Confirm recent long-run distance, duration and recovery before approving marathon long-run progression.

```json
    "note": "The dated handover is provisional. Confirm actual event recovery and completed training before accepting the next heavy or specific block; elapsed days alone do not clear fatigue."
  },
  "missedSession": "Skip or move one session only if the next key session and recovery still fit. Do not stack missed quality, heavy work or long runs. After two missed sessions, illness or a week away, review the next block.",
  "liftingEffort": "Warm up to the prescribed effort. RPE governs the suggested kg. If effort overshoots by a point, reduce 2.5–5% and reassess; if still excessive, omit remaining back-offs. Heavy singles are skill practice, not forced maximal attempts.",
  "speedWork": "Accelerate with stable technique. Reduce load or stop when speed or position deteriorates. An easy speed day is not permission to turn it into a heavy day.",
  "athleteJudgement": "Report sustained fatigue, sleep disruption, pain, illness or altered movement. End the provoking work when needed. One difficult session is a reason to adjust that session, not an automatic full deload.",
  "review": "Review completed work every four weeks and after events. Later blocks are conditional on observed recovery and performance; no race result or lifting total is guaranteed."
}
```

```json
{
  "handoverRecovery": {
    "eventDate": "2027-01-23",
    "sport": "strength",
    "conditional": true,
    "note": "The dated handover is provisional. Confirm actual event recovery and completed training before accepting the next heavy or specific block; elapsed days alone do not clear fatigue."
  },
  "missedSession": "Skip or move one session only if the next key session and recovery still fit. Do not stack missed quality, heavy work or long runs. After two missed sessions, illness or a week away, review the next block.",
  "liftingEffort": "Warm up to the prescribed effort. RPE governs the suggested kg. If effort overshoots by a point, reduce 2.5–5% and reassess; if still excessive, omit remaining back-offs. Heavy singles are skill practice, not forced maximal attempts.",
  "speedWork": "Accelerate with stable technique. Reduce load or stop when speed or position deteriorates. An easy speed day is not permission to turn it into a heavy day.",
  "athleteJudgement": "Report sustained fatigue, sleep disruption, pain, illness or altered movement. End the provoking work when needed. One difficult session is a reason to adjust that session, not an automatic full deload.",
  "review": "Review completed work every four weeks and after events. Later blocks are conditional on observed recovery and performance; no race result or lifting total is guaranteed."
}
```

## Week 21 — Development / Specific preparation

Priority: Running. Calendar feasible: true. Review required: true.

- Post-race / post-event handover review: confirm actual recovery and completed training before accepting the next heavy or specific block. Elapsed recovery days alone do not establish readiness.
- The post-meet marathon build is conditional on restored recovery and completed aerobic support. Confirm actual mileage at the handover before progressing; proposed future kilometres are not evidence that those runs were completed.
- Confirm recent long-run distance, duration and recovery before approving marathon long-run progression.
- This long-run rehearsal exceeds 3.5 hours. Its use is conditional on recovery, fuelling tolerance and an agreed visit window; review duration versus distance instead of forcing 30 km or a fixed percentage.

## Week 23 — Development / Specific preparation

Priority: Running. Calendar feasible: true. Review required: true.

- Post-race / post-event handover review: confirm actual recovery and completed training before accepting the next heavy or specific block. Elapsed recovery days alone do not establish readiness.
- The post-meet marathon build is conditional on restored recovery and completed aerobic support. Confirm actual mileage at the handover before progressing; proposed future kilometres are not evidence that those runs were completed.
- Long-run duration/history limits redistribute distance into supporting runs. Confirm those visits fit; do not silently add distance to the long run.
- Confirm recent long-run distance, duration and recovery before approving marathon long-run progression.
- This long-run rehearsal exceeds 3.5 hours. Its use is conditional on recovery, fuelling tolerance and an agreed visit window; review duration versus distance instead of forcing 30 km or a fixed percentage.

```json
  "athleteJudgement": "Report sustained fatigue, sleep disruption, pain, illness or altered movement. End the provoking work when needed. One difficult session is a reason to adjust that session, not an automatic full deload.",

}
```

```json
  "liftingEffort": "Warm up to the prescribed effort. RPE governs the suggested kg. If effort overshoots by a point, reduce 2.5–5% and reassess; if still excessive, omit remaining back-offs. Heavy singles are skill practice, not forced maximal attempts.",
```
